Well, I guess I'm here because things at the transit authority have been... well, they've been coming after me. It really started ever since I won that county safety award. Which, honestly, they just hand you this cheap little glass plaque. I actually had to go to Home Depot to buy a special floating shelf just to put it up in my apartment, and you know how the screws they give you in those mounting kits strip out almost immediately? I had to dig through my toolbox to find my own drywall anchors. I spent like two hours on a Sunday just hanging the stupid thing. 

But anyway, ever since I got that award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports. I mainly inspect the articulated buses, you know, the really long ones with the accordion middle? Those things are an absolute nightmare in the snow. High? Like, euphoric? No, definitely not. I mean, I'm just trying to keep my head down and do my job. The only thing keeping me going is coffee, honestly. Voices? No, no, I don't hear anything like that. Just the usual ringing in my ears, honestly. I've had that ever since I started working around the air brakes on the buses. But they never stay in right. I bought a pack of fifty off Amazon and I swear half of them ended up in my washing machine because I kept forgetting to take them out of my pockets. My landlord actually got mad at me because he thought I broke the agitator in the building's washer, but it was just a loose belt. I ended up fixing it myself because he's completely useless. Took me like ten minutes with a socket wrench.

Just the usual ringing in my ears, honestly. I've had that ever since I started working around the air brakes on the buses. They tell you to wear the ear protection, but those big earmuff things they issue us get so sweaty in the summer, it's disgusting. I tried buying those little foam plugs, the orange ones? But they never stay in right. I bought a pack of fifty off Amazon and I swear half of them ended up in my washing machine because I kept forgetting to take them out of my pockets. My landlord actually got mad at me because he thought I broke the agitator in the building's washer, but it was just a loose belt. I ended up fixing it myself because he's completely useless. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Control my thoughts? No, I mean, nobody's doing anything sci-fi like that. Monitor me... well, they definitely watch my reports like a hawk, but not with special devices. Just their own eyes. Honestly, the technology they have at the depot is so outdated, I doubt they could monitor a toaster. We still use these clunky old Panasonic Toughbooks for our field diagnostics. The batteries on those things are completely shot. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. 

I actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. Which is ridiculous, because how am I supposed to run a full diagnostic on a transmission when the computer dies halfway through the scan? I end up having to run this massive extension cord across the garage floor, which is a huge tripping hazard. OSHA would have an absolute field day if they saw it. I actually took a picture of it on my phone just in case someone trips and tries to blame me for it. You really have to cover your own behind these days, you know? The cameras in the garage don't even point at that bay anyway, they're all angled toward the tool crib because someone was stealing impact wrenches last year. 

But yeah, no mind control or anything crazy like that. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.

The cameras in the garage don't even point at that bay anyway, they're all angled toward the tool crib because someone was stealing impact wrenches last year. \n\nBut yeah, no mind control or anything crazy like that. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHas anyone shown you clear proof that your supervisor’s actions are just a routine review, and if so, do you still believe they’re trying to discredit you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 28 / transportation_inspector / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because ever since that county safety award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports and asking pointed budget questions like they’re trying to make me look crooked, and even when my supervisor says it’s just normal review, I can tell there’s a coordinated effort to discredit me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Proof? I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. 

Anyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. 

So yeah, he can call it a "normal review" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.

I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 28 / transportation_inspector / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. 

It was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. 

But yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, "Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines," but he just ignores me. 

He actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. 

But anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.

I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. \n\nIt was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. \n\nBut yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems at work started, did you have any recent head injury, seizure, or serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. 

I was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, "Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt." I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. 

But yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.

They tell you to wear the ear protection, but those big earmuff things they issue us get so sweaty in the summer, it's disgusting. I tried buying those little foam plugs, the orange ones? But they never stay in right. I bought a pack of fifty off Amazon and I swear half of them ended up in my washing machine because I kept forgetting to take them out of my pockets. My landlord actually got mad at me because he thought I broke the agitator in the building's washer, but it was just a loose belt. I ended up fixing it myself because he's completely useless. Took me like ten minutes with a socket wrench. \n\nBut no, no voices or anything. Just the ringing. And I guess the noise from my upstairs neighbor, he plays the TV way too loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anyone is using special devices or technology to control your thoughts or monitor you?\n\nPatient: Control my thoughts? No, I mean, nobody's doing anything sci-fi like that. Monitor me... well, they definitely watch my reports like a hawk, but not with special devices. Just their own eyes. Honestly, the technology they have at the depot is so outdated, I doubt they could monitor a toaster. We still use these clunky old Panasonic Toughbooks for our field diagnostics. The batteries on those things are completely shot. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. \n\nI actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. Which is ridiculous, because how am I supposed to run a full diagnostic on a transmission when the computer dies halfway through the scan? I end up having to run this massive extension cord across the garage floor, which is a huge tripping hazard. OSHA would have an absolute field day if they saw it. I actually took a picture of it on my phone just in case someone trips and tries to blame me for it. You really have to cover your own behind these days, you know? The cameras in the garage don't even point at that bay anyway, they're all angled toward the tool crib because someone was stealing impact wrenches last year. \n\nBut yeah, no mind control or anything crazy like that. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone shown you clear proof that your supervisor’s actions are just a routine review, and if so, do you still believe they’re trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Proof? I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. \n\nIt was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. \n\nBut yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems at work started, did you have any recent head injury, seizure, or serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. 

But yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.

No, I mean, nobody's doing anything sci-fi like that. Monitor me... well, they definitely watch my reports like a hawk, but not with special devices. Just their own eyes. Honestly, the technology they have at the depot is so outdated, I doubt they could monitor a toaster. We still use these clunky old Panasonic Toughbooks for our field diagnostics. The batteries on those things are completely shot. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. \n\nI actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. \n\nIt was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. \n\nBut yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems at work started, did you have any recent head injury, seizure, or serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this issue at work, have you been having trouble managing your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. \n\nBut yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this issue at work, have you been having trouble managing your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. \n\nBut yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\n[Last Question]\nOutside of your concerns about people at work trying to discredit you, have you had any other beliefs that others say are strange or hard to believe?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 28 / transportation_inspector / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Other beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. I mean, my buddy Craig thinks it's hard to believe that I still use a manual push mower for my lawn, but that's just a preference. Not the gas kind, the old-school reel mower with the spinning blades. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. 

Plus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. You had to push that little red primer bulb exactly three times—not two, not four—or the whole engine would flood and you'd be yanking that pull-cord for an hour. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. 

But anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.

But they never stay in right. I bought a pack of fifty off Amazon and I swear half of them ended up in my washing machine because I kept forgetting to take them out of my pockets. No, I mean, nobody's doing anything sci-fi like that. Monitor me... well, they definitely watch my reports like a hawk, but not with special devices. Just their own eyes. Honestly, the technology they have at the depot is so outdated, I doubt they could monitor a toaster. We still use these clunky old Panasonic Toughbooks for our field diagnostics. The batteries on those things are completely shot. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. \n\nI actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. Which is ridiculous, because how am I supposed to run a full diagnostic on a transmission when the computer dies halfway through the scan? I end up having to run this massive extension cord across the garage floor, which is a huge tripping hazard. OSHA would have an absolute field day if they saw it. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone shown you clear proof that your supervisor’s actions are just a routine review, and if so, do you still believe they’re trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Proof? I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. \n\nIt was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. \n\nBut yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems at work started, did you have any recent head injury, seizure, or serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this issue at work, have you been having trouble managing your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. \n\nBut yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about people at work trying to discredit you, have you had any other beliefs that others say are strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Other beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. I mean, my buddy Craig thinks it's hard to believe that I still use a manual push mower for my lawn, but that's just a preference. Not the gas kind, the old-school reel mower with the spinning blades. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. \n\nPlus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. You had to push that little red primer bulb exactly three times—not two, not four—or the whole engine would flood and you'd be yanking that pull-cord for an hour. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that when you talk, your thoughts seem hard to follow or go off track?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. I mean, I think I'm pretty clear when I talk. People usually understand me just fine. Except maybe my sister, but she never really listens anyway. She's always on her phone, scrolling through those short videos, you know? The ones with the weird dances and the loud music. \n\nI don't even have that app on my phone. I try to keep my phone pretty clean, just the basics—maps, weather, maybe a few podcasts. I've been listening to this one podcast about the history of the interstate highway system. It's actually fascinating. You wouldn't think concrete and asphalt could be that interesting, but the politics behind where they put the exits in the 1950s is wild. Like, they literally routed entire highways just to avoid certain neighborhoods, or to benefit specific politicians who owned land nearby. It's all about who you know, right? Just like at work. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think my thoughts are hard to follow at all. I just like to give the full picture, you know? You can't really understand a situation unless you have all the details. So no, no one's ever told me I go off track.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about people at work trying to discredit you, have you had any other beliefs that others say are strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Other beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. I mean, my buddy Craig thinks it's hard to believe that I still use a manual push mower for my lawn, but that's just a preference. Not the gas kind, the old-school reel mower with the spinning blades. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. \n\nPlus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that when you talk, your thoughts seem hard to follow or go off track?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. I mean, I think I'm pretty clear when I talk. People usually understand me just fine. Except maybe my sister, but she never really listens anyway. She's always on her phone, scrolling through those short videos, you know? The ones with the weird dances and the loud music. \n\nI don't even have that app on my phone. I try to keep my phone pretty clean, just the basics—maps, weather, maybe a few podcasts. I've been listening to this one podcast about the history of the interstate highway system. Like, they literally routed entire highways just to avoid certain neighborhoods, or to benefit specific politicians who owned land nearby. It's all about who you know, right? Just like at work. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think my thoughts are hard to follow at all. I just like to give the full picture, you know? You can't really understand a situation unless you have all the details. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mistaken? No, there's zero chance I'm mistaken. I know exactly what I'm seeing. It all started right after I got that county safety award. Which, by the way, was just this cheap particle-board plaque. You’d think for a county-wide recognition they could at least spring for solid wood, like oak or maple, right? My uncle actually used to do a lot of woodworking, mostly custom cabinets down in Florida. He made an absolute killing during the housing boom in the early 2000s. He even bought this massive 30-foot center console boat, and we’d take it out fishing for mahi-mahi. Best fish you’ll ever eat, if you just grill it with a little lemon and butter. You have to be careful not to overcook it though, or it gets really rubbery, almost like chewing on a tire. 

But anyway, yeah, ever since I got that award, certain people at work have been completely twisting my inspection reports. And they keep asking these really pointed budget questions, like they’re trying to make me look crooked or something. Which is crazy because I keep track of every single penny. Speaking of pennies, did you know it actually costs more to make a penny than a penny is worth? It's like two cents in zinc and copper just to mint one. I read they might phase them out entirely, which makes sense because you can't even buy a piece of gum with a penny anymore. I miss when you could get those little Bazooka Joe gums with the tiny comic strips inside. I used to collect those when I was a kid.

But my supervisor, he keeps trying to brush it off. He tells me it’s just a normal review, just standard procedure for the department. But I'm not an idiot. I can tell. There is a coordinated effort to discredit me, 100 percent. It's not a mistake, it's exactly what's happening.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because things at the transit authority have been... well, they've been coming after me. It really started ever since I won that county safety award. Which, honestly, they just hand you this cheap little glass plaque. I actually had to go to Home Depot to buy a special floating shelf just to put it up in my apartment, and you know how the screws they give you in those mounting kits strip out almost immediately? I had to dig through my toolbox to find my own drywall anchors. I spent like two hours on a Sunday just hanging the stupid thing. \n\nBut anyway, ever since I got that award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions out of nowhere, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. I mainly inspect the articulated buses, you know, the really long ones with the accordion middle? Those things are an absolute nightmare in the snow. Last winter one jackknifed on 4th street and blocked traffic for six hours, it was on the local news. \n\nBut yeah, my supervisor keeps telling me, oh, it’s just a normal review process, don't worry about it. But I'm not an idiot. I can tell there’s a coordinated effort to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: High? Like, euphoric? No, definitely not. I mean, I'm just trying to keep my head down and do my job. The only thing keeping me going is coffee, honestly. Though I actually had to stop going to the Dunkin' near the depot because the drive-thru line spills out right onto the main road. It's a massive safety hazard. I actually wrote up a hazard report about it for the city traffic division on my own time, because people just sit there blocking the right lane. If a fire truck or an ambulance needed to get through, they'd be completely stuck. \n\nIt’s ridiculous how poorly zoned that whole intersection is, especially with the new apartment complex they just built across the street. The construction dirt from that place was coating all the buses in the yard for months, we had to run them through the wash bay twice as often. The brushes in the wash bay are already on their last legs anyway... \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, euphoric. No, I don't feel anything like that. I just feel normal. Well, normal except for the stress of them messing with my reports.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anything like that. Just the usual ringing in my ears, honestly. I've had that ever since I started working around the air brakes on the buses. They tell you to wear the ear protection, but those big earmuff things they issue us get so sweaty in the summer, it's disgusting. I tried buying those little foam plugs, the orange ones? But they never stay in right. I bought a pack of fifty off Amazon and I swear half of them ended up in my washing machine because I kept forgetting to take them out of my pockets. My landlord actually got mad at me because he thought I broke the agitator in the building's washer, but it was just a loose belt. I ended up fixing it myself because he's completely useless. Took me like ten minutes with a socket wrench. \n\nBut no, no voices or anything. Just the ringing. And I guess the noise from my upstairs neighbor, he plays the TV way too loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anyone is using special devices or technology to control your thoughts or monitor you?\n\nPatient: Control my thoughts? No, I mean, nobody's doing anything sci-fi like that. Monitor me... well, they definitely watch my reports like a hawk, but not with special devices. Just their own eyes. Honestly, the technology they have at the depot is so outdated, I doubt they could monitor a toaster. We still use these clunky old Panasonic Toughbooks for our field diagnostics. The batteries on those things are completely shot. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. \n\nI actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. Which is ridiculous, because how am I supposed to run a full diagnostic on a transmission when the computer dies halfway through the scan? I end up having to run this massive extension cord across the garage floor, which is a huge tripping hazard. OSHA would have an absolute field day if they saw it. I actually took a picture of it on my phone just in case someone trips and tries to blame me for it. You really have to cover your own behind these days, you know? The cameras in the garage don't even point at that bay anyway, they're all angled toward the tool crib because someone was stealing impact wrenches last year. \n\nBut yeah, no mind control or anything crazy like that. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone shown you clear proof that your supervisor’s actions are just a routine review, and if so, do you still believe they’re trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Proof? I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. \n\nIt was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. \n\nBut yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems at work started, did you have any recent head injury, seizure, or serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this issue at work, have you been having trouble managing your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. \n\nBut yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about people at work trying to discredit you, have you had any other beliefs that others say are strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Other beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. I mean, my buddy Craig thinks it's hard to believe that I still use a manual push mower for my lawn, but that's just a preference. Not the gas kind, the old-school reel mower with the spinning blades. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. \n\nPlus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. You had to push that little red primer bulb exactly three times—not two, not four—or the whole engine would flood and you'd be yanking that pull-cord for an hour. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that when you talk, your thoughts seem hard to follow or go off track?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. I mean, I think I'm pretty clear when I talk. People usually understand me just fine. Except maybe my sister, but she never really listens anyway. She's always on her phone, scrolling through those short videos, you know? The ones with the weird dances and the loud music. \n\nI don't even have that app on my phone. I try to keep my phone pretty clean, just the basics—maps, weather, maybe a few podcasts. I've been listening to this one podcast about the history of the interstate highway system.
