My landlord actually got mad at me because he thought I broke the agitator in the building's washer, but it was just a loose belt. I ended up fixing it myself because he's completely useless. Took me like ten minutes with a socket wrench. \n\nBut no, no voices or anything. Just the ringing. And I guess the noise from my upstairs neighbor, he plays the TV way too loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anyone is using special devices or technology to control your thoughts or monitor you?\n\nPatient: Control my thoughts? No, I mean, nobody's doing anything sci-fi like that. Monitor me... well, they definitely watch my reports like a hawk, but not with special devices. Just their own eyes. Honestly, the technology they have at the depot is so outdated, I doubt they could monitor a toaster. We still use these clunky old Panasonic Toughbooks for our field diagnostics. The batteries on those things are completely shot. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. \n\nI actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. Which is ridiculous, because how am I supposed to run a full diagnostic on a transmission when the computer dies halfway through the scan? I end up having to run this massive extension cord across the garage floor, which is a huge tripping hazard. OSHA would have an absolute field day if they saw it. I actually took a picture of it on my phone just in case someone trips and tries to blame me for it. You really have to cover your own behind these days, you know? The cameras in the garage don't even point at that bay anyway, they're all angled toward the tool crib because someone was stealing impact wrenches last year. \n\nBut yeah, no mind control or anything crazy like that. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone shown you clear proof that your supervisor’s actions are just a routine review, and if so, do you still believe they’re trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Proof? I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I mean, I sleep the same amount I always have. Usually around six or seven hours, depending on how hot my apartment gets. The radiator in my bedroom is completely broken, so it just blasts heat all winter. I've complained to the landlord like five times, but like I said, the guy is totally useless. I actually had to crack my window open in January just to get some decent airflow, and then a pigeon tried to build a nest on my window sill. \n\nIt was the most annoying thing. Every morning at like 5:00 AM, just *coo, coo, coo*. I tried putting out those little plastic spikes, you know, the ones they use on building ledges? I got them at Home Depot. The guy in the garden center told me they wouldn't work on pigeons because pigeons are stubborn, and he was totally right. The bird just started weaving its twigs right through the spikes. It actually made the nest stronger, if you think about it. I ended up having to buy this fake plastic owl to scare it away, which actually worked, but now I just have a creepy plastic owl staring at me while I try to sleep. \n\nBut yeah, my sleep is fine. I don't feel like I need less of it or anything. I just wish I didn't have to deal with the heating situation.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems at work started, did you have any recent head injury, seizure, or serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: Head injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this issue at work, have you been having trouble managing your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. \n\nBut yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about people at work trying to discredit you, have you had any other beliefs that others say are strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Other beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. I mean, my buddy Craig thinks it's hard to believe that I still use a manual push mower for my lawn, but that's just a preference. Not the gas kind, the old-school reel mower with the spinning blades. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. \n\nPlus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. You had to push that little red primer bulb exactly three times—not two, not four—or the whole engine would flood and you'd be yanking that pull-cord for an hour. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that when you talk, your thoughts seem hard to follow or go off track?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. I mean, I think I'm pretty clear when I talk. People usually understand me just fine. Except maybe my sister, but she never really listens anyway. She's always on her phone, scrolling through those short videos, you know? The ones with the weird dances and the loud music. \n\nI don't even have that app on my phone. I try to keep my phone pretty clean, just the basics—maps, weather, maybe a few podcasts. I've been listening to this one podcast about the history of the interstate highway system. It's actually fascinating. You wouldn't think concrete and asphalt could be that interesting, but the politics behind where they put the exits in the 1950s is wild. Like, they literally routed entire highways just to avoid certain neighborhoods, or to benefit specific politicians who owned land nearby. It's all about who you know, right? Just like at work. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think my thoughts are hard to follow at all. I just like to give the full picture, you know? You can't really understand a situation unless you have all the details. So no, no one's ever told me I go off track.\n\nDoctor: Is it possible that you might be mistaken about your coworkers trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, there's zero chance I'm mistaken. I know exactly what I'm seeing. It all started right after I got that county safety award. Which, by the way, was just this cheap particle-board plaque. You’d think for a county-wide recognition they could at least spring for solid wood, like oak or maple, right? My uncle actually used to do a lot of woodworking, mostly custom cabinets down in Florida. He made an absolute killing during the housing boom in the early 2000s. He even bought this massive 30-foot center console boat, and we’d take it out fishing for mahi-mahi. Best fish you’ll ever eat, if you just grill it with a little lemon and butter. You have to be careful not to overcook it though, or it gets really rubbery, almost like chewing on a tire. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, ever since I got that award, certain people at work have been completely twisting my inspection reports. And they keep asking these really pointed budget questions, like they’re trying to make me look crooked or something. Which is crazy because I keep track of every single penny. Speaking of pennies, did you know it actually costs more to make a penny than a penny is worth? It's like two cents in zinc and copper just to mint one. I read they might phase them out entirely, which makes sense because you can't even buy a piece of gum with a penny anymore. I miss when you could get those little Bazooka Joe gums with the tiny comic strips inside. I used to collect those when I was a kid.\n\nBut my supervisor, he keeps trying to brush it off. He tells me it’s just a normal review, just standard procedure for the department. But I'm not an idiot. I can tell. There is a coordinated effort to discredit me, 100 percent. It's not a mistake, it's exactly what's happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Impossible? No, not impossible. I mean, they're just changing my reports and asking weird questions. It's not like they're, I don't know, reading my mind or using lasers or something crazy like that. It's just office politics, plain and simple. Just like how they changed the whole filing system last year. We used to use the color-coded manila folders—red for urgent, yellow for pending, green for completed. It made perfect sense. You could just glance at a stack and know exactly what you were dealing with. 

But then they brought in this new software system, supposedly to go "paperless." Let me tell you, we use more paper now than we ever did because everybody just prints out the digital files anyway so they can actually read them. Staring at a screen all day is terrible for your eyes. My optometrist told me I need to start wearing those blue-light blocking glasses, but I think they look ridiculous. They have this weird yellowish tint to them, makes you look like you're wearing safety goggles from shop class. I took shop class in high school, actually built a pretty nice birdhouse. It was supposed to be a spice rack, but the measurements got a little messed up on the table saw. Table saws are incredibly dangerous if you don't know what you're doing. A guy I used to know, Frank, he lost the tip of his index finger to a table saw. Just zip, right off. He said it didn't even hurt at first, just felt really cold. 

But yeah, no, nothing impossible. They're just doing normal, sneaky office stuff to try and ruin my reputation. It's completely realistic, unfortunately.

We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a year now. Yeah, right around a year, ever since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. It was a pretty big deal, honestly. They had this whole ceremony at the community center downtown. The parking there is an absolute nightmare, by the way. They only have this tiny lot behind the building, and half the spaces are reserved for city vehicles, which are never even there! So I had to park three blocks away and walk in the rain. \n\nI was wearing my good suit, too. I only have one, I got it at Men's Wearhouse for my cousin's wedding a few years back. The guy who measured me was super pushy about selling me a tie clip. He just kept bringing out different ones, like, \"Oh, this silver one really brings out the blue in the shirt.\" I don't even like tie clips, they always feel like they're choking the tie, you know? Plus, I was already spending enough on the suit and the shoes. Shoes are ridiculously expensive now. I saw a pair of sneakers at the mall the other day going for like two hundred dollars. Who pays two hundred dollars for sneakers? You're just gonna walk on the ground with them and get them dirty anyway. \n\nBut yeah, ever since that safety initiative award, about a year ago, that's when all this started. That's when I noticed the reports being twisted and the weird questions.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this issue at work, have you been having trouble managing your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: No, my day-to-day life is totally fine. I mean, I still get to work on time every morning, do all my inspections, and I still go to church on Sundays. Our pastor actually just started this new sermon series on the Book of Daniel, which is interesting, but the acoustics in our sanctuary are just terrible. It's this old building from the fifties, and they put in this new sound system a couple of years ago, but whoever installed it didn't know what they were doing. If you sit anywhere in the back three pews, there's this constant, low-level humming noise coming from the speakers. It drives me absolutely crazy. I mentioned it to the head usher, Dave, but he's practically deaf in one ear from working at a stamping plant for thirty years, so he doesn't hear it. Dave's a good guy, though. He always brings these little hard candies and hands them out to the kids. I think they're butterscotch. I haven't had a butterscotch candy in probably ten years. They always remind me of my grandma's house, she kept them in this weird glass dish that looked like a swan. \n\nBut yeah, I take care of myself, pay my bills, all that normal stuff. The only time things get really stressful or I have problems is when people start bringing up those safety reports at work, or when they start questioning my spending on the initiative. That's when it all flares up. But otherwise, I'm managing everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about people at work trying to discredit you, have you had any other beliefs that others say are strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Other beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. I mean, my buddy Craig thinks it's hard to believe that I still use a manual push mower for my lawn, but that's just a preference. Not the gas kind, the old-school reel mower with the spinning blades. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. \n\nPlus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. You had to push that little red primer bulb exactly three times—not two, not four—or the whole engine would flood and you'd be yanking that pull-cord for an hour. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that when you talk, your thoughts seem hard to follow or go off track?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. I mean, I think I'm pretty clear when I talk. People usually understand me just fine. Except maybe my sister, but she never really listens anyway. She's always on her phone, scrolling through those short videos, you know? The ones with the weird dances and the loud music. \n\nI don't even have that app on my phone. I try to keep my phone pretty clean, just the basics—maps, weather, maybe a few podcasts. I've been listening to this one podcast about the history of the interstate highway system. It's actually fascinating. You wouldn't think concrete and asphalt could be that interesting, but the politics behind where they put the exits in the 1950s is wild. Like, they literally routed entire highways just to avoid certain neighborhoods, or to benefit specific politicians who owned land nearby. It's all about who you know, right? Just like at work. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think my thoughts are hard to follow at all. I just like to give the full picture, you know? You can't really understand a situation unless you have all the details. So no, no one's ever told me I go off track.\n\nDoctor: Is it possible that you might be mistaken about your coworkers trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, there's zero chance I'm mistaken. I know exactly what I'm seeing. It all started right after I got that county safety award. Which, by the way, was just this cheap particle-board plaque. You’d think for a county-wide recognition they could at least spring for solid wood, like oak or maple, right? My uncle actually used to do a lot of woodworking, mostly custom cabinets down in Florida. He made an absolute killing during the housing boom in the early 2000s. He even bought this massive 30-foot center console boat, and we’d take it out fishing for mahi-mahi. Best fish you’ll ever eat, if you just grill it with a little lemon and butter. You have to be careful not to overcook it though, or it gets really rubbery, almost like chewing on a tire. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, ever since I got that award, certain people at work have been completely twisting my inspection reports. And they keep asking these really pointed budget questions, like they’re trying to make me look crooked or something. Which is crazy because I keep track of every single penny. Speaking of pennies, did you know it actually costs more to make a penny than a penny is worth? It's like two cents in zinc and copper just to mint one. I read they might phase them out entirely, which makes sense because you can't even buy a piece of gum with a penny anymore. I miss when you could get those little Bazooka Joe gums with the tiny comic strips inside. I used to collect those when I was a kid.\n\nBut my supervisor, he keeps trying to brush it off. He tells me it’s just a normal review, just standard procedure for the department. But I'm not an idiot. I can tell. There is a coordinated effort to discredit me, 100 percent. It's not a mistake, it's exactly what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your coworkers are trying to discredit you ever included anything that would seem impossible to other people?\n\nPatient: Impossible? No, not impossible. I mean, they're just changing my reports and asking weird questions. It's not like they're, I don't know, reading my mind or using lasers or something crazy like that. It's just office politics, plain and simple. Just like how they changed the whole filing system last year. We used to use the color-coded manila folders—red for urgent, yellow for pending, green for completed. It made perfect sense. You could just glance at a stack and know exactly what you were dealing with. \n\nBut then they brought in this new software system, supposedly to go \"paperless.\" Let me tell you, we use more paper now than we ever did because everybody just prints out the digital files anyway so they can actually read them. Staring at a screen all day is terrible for your eyes. My optometrist told me I need to start wearing those blue-light blocking glasses, but I think they look ridiculous. They have this weird yellowish tint to them, makes you look like you're wearing safety goggles from shop class. I took shop class in high school, actually built a pretty nice birdhouse. It was supposed to be a spice rack, but the measurements got a little messed up on the table saw. Table saws are incredibly dangerous if you don't know what you're doing. A guy I used to know, Frank, he lost the tip of his index finger to a table saw. Just zip, right off. He said it didn't even hurt at first, just felt really cold. \n\nBut yeah, no, nothing impossible. They're just doing normal, sneaky office stuff to try and ruin my reputation. It's completely realistic, unfortunately."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nWell, I guess I'm here because things at the transit authority have been... well, they've been coming after me. It really started ever since I won that county safety award. Which, honestly, they just hand you this cheap little glass plaque. I actually had to go to Home Depot to buy a special floating shelf just to put it up in my apartment, and you know how the screws they give you in those mounting kits strip out almost immediately? I had to dig through my toolbox to find my own drywall anchors. I spent like two hours on a Sunday just hanging the stupid thing. \n\nBut anyway, ever since I got that award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions out of nowhere, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. I mainly inspect the articulated buses, you know, the really long ones with the accordion middle? Those things are an absolute nightmare in the snow. Last winter one jackknifed on 4th street and blocked traffic for six hours, it was on the local news. \n\nBut yeah, my supervisor keeps telling me, oh, it’s just a normal review process, don't worry about it. But I'm not an idiot. I can tell there’s a coordinated effort to discredit me.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"

Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment. If you unplug it from the charger in the truck, you have maybe four minutes before the screen just goes black. \n\nI actually put in a requisition order for new batteries back in March, and the procurement guy—Dave, I think his name is—he rejected it because he said we had to wait for the new fiscal year. Which is ridiculous, because how am I supposed to run a full diagnostic on a transmission when the computer dies halfway through the scan? I end up having to run this massive extension cord across the garage floor, which is a huge tripping hazard. OSHA would have an absolute field day if they saw it. I actually took a picture of it on my phone just in case someone trips and tries to blame me for it. You really have to cover your own behind these days, you know? The cameras in the garage don't even point at that bay anyway, they're all angled toward the tool crib because someone was stealing impact wrenches last year. \n\nBut yeah, no mind control or anything crazy like that. Just the usual people at work trying to find a reason to fire me.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nProof? I mean, my supervisor *says* it's just a routine review. He pulled me into his office last week—actually, it was Tuesday, because that was the day the vending machine in the break room ate my dollar. I just wanted a pack of peanut butter crackers, the orange ones, you know? But the coil turned halfway and the crackers just hung there. I tried shaking the machine, which you're not supposed to do, obviously, there's a sticker right on the glass that says it can tip over and crush you. Did you know vending machines kill more people a year than sharks? I read that somewhere. Probably on Reddit. I waste way too much time on my phone when I'm waiting for the air pressure to build up in the brake lines during an inspection. \n\nAnyway, he pulled me in and gave me this whole speech about how auditing the reports is standard procedure now. But I know better. It all started right after I won that county safety award. Ever since I got that plaque, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports, asking these really pointed questions about the budget, like they're trying to make me look crooked or something. Like I'm taking kickbacks from the brake pad supplier! Which is absurd, because the supplier they use is terrible. The pads squeak right out of the box. I've told them a hundred times we need to switch to the ceramic ones, but nobody listens to me. \n\nSo yeah, he can call it a \"normal review\" all he wants, but I can tell. There's a coordinated effort to discredit me. They just want me out of the way.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nHead injury? Seizures? No, nothing like that. I mean, I'm pretty healthy. I did bump my head on the side mirror of a Freightliner back in October, but that doesn't count. Those mirrors stick out way further than you think, especially when they fold them in wrong. The driver, this guy Gary, he always parked his rig crooked in the inspection bay. I told him a million times, \"Gary, you gotta use the painted yellow lines,\" but he just ignores me. \n\nHe actually backed over a traffic cone once and dragged it for like three miles on the highway before another trucker radioed him. It was honestly hilarious, but also a huge safety violation. The cone was completely shredded by the time he pulled over, just orange plastic ribbons. We had to order a whole new stack of them from Uline. Have you ever looked through a Uline catalog? They send these massive books to the depot, like old-school phone books. I don't know why they still print them, it seems like a massive waste of paper when everyone just orders online anyway. Plus they weigh like five pounds, so if you drop one on your foot, you're definitely feeling it. They used to give away free stuff if you ordered enough, like a cooler or a cheap jacket, but I think they stopped doing that. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, just a little bump from the truck mirror. No concussions or seizures or any major medical stuff. I'm fine physically.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli. Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nOther beliefs? No, definitely not. Everything else is just normal life. It's actually way better for the grass, it cuts it clean like scissors instead of tearing it. \n\nPlus, gas mowers are just a massive hassle. You have to mess with the oil, and the spark plugs always get fouled up if you leave them sitting in the shed over the winter. My dad had this old Toro mower when I was a kid, and it was a total nightmare to start. You had to push that little red primer bulb exactly three times—not two, not four—or the whole engine would flood and you'd be yanking that pull-cord for an hour. We had this huge yard, too, almost an acre, out by the old water tower. They actually tore that tower down a few years back to build a strip mall, which is a real shame because it was kind of a historical landmark for the county. Now it's just a mattress store and a chain sub shop. I really don't get how mattress stores stay in business, honestly. You buy a mattress what, maybe once every ten years? How are they paying rent on a building that big? There's literally never anyone in the parking lot when I drive by. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't have any strange beliefs or anything like that. I see things exactly how they are. It's really just the stuff at work that's the issue.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate.
